**2026-08-01 (later session) — H17 MERGED (PR #78 `aa38a58`, squash, 6/6 CI green on the first run); H1f BUILT on branch `h1f-workflow-branching-design` — the workflow/branching design doc → Doc #27; H-map row H1f (docs, S, no deps).** The session opened by merging H17 unattended once `gh` was authenticated from the git credential helper (the recorded recipe; `gh` is never pre-authed here), then applied every H-map blocker to name the next move rather than picking one: H1c + H20a/b/c are cut to Phase 4, H16a/b/c are deferred to Phase 3b, and H1d — and therefore H18a/b + H19a/b — is blocked on the user's OCR document sample, leaving H1e, H1f, H22a/b, H23a/b and H25. Of those, **H1f is the only row that is another row's missing dependency**: the H-map's own ordering paired H17 and H21a as the two rows H6a unblocked, so with H17 done, H21a is next and H1f gates it — the same just-in-time shape as H1g→H15a and H1h→H6a. **The increment turned out to be much larger than its "docs, S" row.** Writing an accurate as-built section meant reading the step model rather than the spec, and the step model is entirely emergent: there is no `steps` table, no edge table and no successor column anywhere — a "step" is a client-side computed projection over `(sections by sequence, filtered by relevance)`, and navigation is index arithmetic over that array. Ratifying that as the design was easy; **the four behaviours it leaves undefined were not**, and finding them turned up **nine defects, five of them live on `main` today**. The two worth remembering: `toSemanticInput()` never passes a clock, so `today()`/`now()` resolve to ABSENT in the guest SPA while PHP always stamps one — **a live PHP/TS relevance divergence that the R3 golden gate structurally cannot catch, because the golden runner supplies `now` from each vector**; and an empty step list makes `isLastStep` evaluate `-1 === -1` → true, so the respondent sees "Step 1 of 0" over an empty form with a **live Submit button that succeeds**. Neither was fixed — H1f is docs-only by construction — but each is written down with an H21 slice named as its owner, so the next increment inherits a work-list rather than a surprise. **Three H-map line items were also refuted rather than inherited:** H21a's "frozen into `schema_snapshot`" is a no-op, because the serializer already emits per-section `relevant_expression`/`sequence`/`is_repeatable` and each field's `section_key` — that *is* the step model — so H21a is M and **H21c is the L**, since the Inertia encode path has no client relevance engine at all (`makeSemanticValidator` has zero hits under `resources/js/`); and H21d's "emit ASTs via `StructuredRuleLowering`" is not implementable, because that class lowers validation rows one-directionally and **no AST→expression-string printer exists anywhere in the repo**. **The method that made the difference, and the one to repeat:** every load-bearing claim was grep-verified against source before it became doc text, and two agent-supplied claims were corrected that way (an `ExpressionKind` line range, and a "three places say X" that was two). The corpora were the tripwire throughout and stayed byte-untouched — `grammar_version` at exactly 296 sites, validation at 114, `git diff tests/golden` empty — which is the whole point of an increment that adds no vectors: if that count had moved, the design would have been wrong.

**2026-08-01 (third session) — H1f MERGED (PR #79 `54842c3`, squash, 6/6 CI green on the first run); H21a BUILT on branch `h21a-workflow-branch-backend` — the workflow branch backend; H-map row 251 (`backend, L` there, corrected to M by H1f, shipped M/L).** The session merged H1f unattended and went straight into the increment it exists to unblock, inheriting a work-list rather than a blank sheet: five of H1f's nine recorded defects were assigned here. All five are closed. **The plan changed twice under verification, and both times the verification was the valuable part.** First, `${event_date} >= today()` — Doc #27 §3.4's motivating example for the missing client clock — turned out to be **already always-false in BOTH engines**, because ordering comparison is numeric-only and `Coercion::NUMERIC_RE` rejects an ISO date string; the clock fix is still right, but it restores `= today()`, `count()`, `if()` and `int()`, not date ranges, and §3.4 is amended rather than implemented as written. Second, `ExpressionValidationGate`'s comment asserting that field and section keys are "globally unique per version" is **false** — uniqueness is two independent per-table composite indexes and every app-level enforcer is table-scoped — which turned the planned `count(${section})` seed into something that could silently re-admit a pruned field's answer; the seed ships with a collision guard and the stale comment is corrected. **The single most consequential finding was an R3 one an adversarial read caught before any code was written:** `min_instances` enforcement lives in `processRepeats()`, which has a byte-for-byte TypeScript twin that the golden runners also reach, so the planned PHP-only narrowing would have been a live PHP/TS divergence **invisible to CI**, because every repeat vector in the corpus uses an ordinary `short_text` member. Mirroring it forced `RENDERS_NOTHING` down out of `lib/` into `engine/field-roles.ts` and repointed H17's `PdfFieldRoleTest` regex at the new home. Four decisions were locked with the user: the mirror pair is a **shared behavioural fixture, not the prescribed source-parsing test** (which was rejected as vacuous — that device works for `PdfFieldRole` because it parses actual data, whereas a projection's rule is control flow, so parsing it pins only predicate names); the `min_instances` narrowing is mirrored in both engines; `count(${section})` is fixed at **both** scopes, since the identical trap existed inside a repeat member's own relevance; and the cycle detector uses **structural** edges (containment + skip) rather than `${key}` references alone, because reference-only SCC misses the commonest real cycle — a section gated on a field it contains — while `chained_fixed_point` must stay silent. All gates green in-container: Pest **1788 (+42), zero failures**, PHPStan L8 at exactly the documented 55 baseline with zero in any touched file, Pint 786, the three lint scripts, `composer audit` clean, vue-tsc both tsconfigs, Vitest 675 passed (2 = the known `token-references` flake, verified isolated), Vite build, `openapi.json` drift-free, corpora byte-untouched at 296/114. **The mutation pass produced this session's standing lesson, and it is a sharper version of the existing one.** Dropping the post-loop mask tightening came back GREEN for two compounding reasons: the assertion had been written as an `if/else` invariant that both the fixed and the broken behaviour satisfy, **and** the settle bound `fields + sections + 2` means the FIELD COUNT decides which phase of an oscillation the loop halts on. Probing the mechanism directly across three field counts showed the fix is a genuine no-op at 3 and 5 fields and load-bearing at 4, where the un-tightened engine returns `sectionRelevance = false` beside `fieldRelevance = true` **and persists that answer**. So: when a mutation comes back green, suspect the test — but suspect the FIXTURE too, and probe the mechanism empirically rather than reasoning about it. Seven mutations in total, each reddening a disjoint set; two pairs (the two `count()` seeds, the two predicate-3 arms) were specifically proven independent rather than redundant.

**2026-08-02 — H21a MERGED (PR #80 `a2a6c65`, squash, 6/6 CI green on the first run); H21b OPEN as PR #81 (6/6 CI green on the first run) — guest-runtime non-linear execution; H-map row 252 (`ui, M` there, shipped M/L).** The session merged H21a unattended once `gh` was authed from `git credential fill`, then took the whole of Doc #27 §9's H21b obligation rather than only the five live defects the prompt named — so §4.2's rescue-by-identity, §5.3's resume-drift resolution, §5.2's respondent-facing-percentage drop and §4.4's step-level retained-answer notice ship here too, and **H21c inherits nothing from H1f**. Three decisions were locked with the user before any code: full §9 scope; the replay version guard reads `client.fetchSchema()` rather than decoding the share token's `vid` claim or extending the mint JSON; and the guard applies to resumed-draft rows too, as a recorded narrowing rather than a `from_draft` flag read by one branch. The store rewrite turned on a diagnosis rather than a patch: the never-seeded `currentStepKey` was not a weak guard but the wrong watcher SOURCE — a membership boolean that is false at t=0 and stays false when steps appear, so it never fired and Next was dead forever — replaced by one `watch(visibleSteps, (next, prev))` that seeds-or-self-heals, rescues by identity, records the visited set and publishes a `StepChange` the DOM-free store hands to the components. `isLastStep` gained its `length > 0` guard and a separate `isTerminal` computed carries the view, because the corrected `isLastStep` is false at zero steps and would otherwise render Next instead of §4.1's single labelled Submit. **Mutation testing found a hole in the DESIGN DOC, not just the suite:** §4.2 says "walk outward" but illustrates only the backward case, and deleting the forward arm reddened zero tests, because no fixture had a vanished step without a predecessor. Two tests were added and the mutation then reddened exactly those two — recorded as amendment B2, which corrects Doc #27's own wording. Twelve mutations, each reddening a distinct set. Also carried: the single-page focus rescue could not live in `SectionView` (a section IS the thing removed, unlike a field whose wrapper survives), so `PageView` tracks the focused section on the way in and `SectionView` stamps `data-section-key`; the repo's first `<TransitionGroup>` omits the standard `position: absolute` on leave so a several-hundred-pixel section never draws over the questions below; and `form-filling-ux-flow.md` §10.1's "no separate announcement for a recount" rule had to be NARROWED, because branching breaks its premise that a navigation always follows to carry the new N. Next = **H21c** (port the step model to the Inertia encode path; the L of the family, and the owner of H1f defect (b)).

**2026-08-02 (later session) — H21b MERGED (PR #81 `6f8845b`, squash, 6/6 CI green on the first run); H21c MERGED (PR #82 `061d1ed`, squash, 6/6 CI green after one red cycle that was a Docker Hub outage on the runner rather than the code) — the encode path's step model; H-map row 253 (`full-stack, M` there, corrected to L by Doc #27 §7, shipped L).** The session merged H21b unattended once `gh` was authed from `git credential fill`, then took the last H21 slice before the canvas. **The increment's shape changed under the first hour of reading, and that was the valuable part.** §7 sized H21c as "give the Inertia application a client relevance engine and a step model it has never had"; what it actually needed was to IMPORT one. `createFormRuntime()` turned out to be pure — `vue`, `../engine`, `../lib/schema-mapping`, `../lib/prefill`, `../lib/uuid`, `../lib/types`, and nothing else — so the encode page mounts the guest SPA's own store and client-side parity became ONE implementation rather than two that agree. That reframing is what let three decisions be locked with the user before any code: mount the store; resolve H7's keyable-hidden asymmetry with a "Reference fields" block OUTSIDE the step list rather than an audience axis on both engines (so the step lists stay byte-identical and §9's parity assertion stays a clean equality); and keep `blocks` as the render source, deferring its PHP↔TS normalizer duplication by name in §10 rather than collapsing it into an already-L increment. **§7's landing order was specified and was followed** — the controller change that tells a keyer what relevance dropped shipped first, as its own bisectable commit, because the `semantic` result was already on `SubmissionResult` and had simply been thrown away. **The parity suite is the piece that needed the most honesty.** Once both channels run the same function, a PHP test that runs both projections and compares them asserts a tautology in a cross-channel costume — exactly the vacuous shape §9's own closing caution warns about — so it asserts the INPUT instead: the encode payload's `version.schema` byte-identical to the guest's, and each fixture case's taken path rebuilt from that shipped snapshot. **Mutation testing produced a green result that mattered for the third H21 slice running.** Handing `StepProjection` the OMITTED-filtered field list reddens nothing, because `EncodeFormPresenter::OMITTED` is a strict subset of `PdfFieldRole::Omitted` — an accident of two lists that answer different questions, enforced nowhere. A test now pins the subset directly. A second trap surfaced while writing the PDF cross-check: reading the stored answer document through the wrong relation yields `[]`, every gate then reads ABSENT, and the check passes for the one case whose branches are all off. **The nine-defect tally was also corrected**: v1.2 claimed all nine closed by H21a and H21b; eight were, and §7's was still live at `SubmissionController.php:42` when this opened. **An adversarial pre-merge review over the finished diff then found three real defects that every suite had been green over, and all three were consequences of the decision to mount the store rather than slips**: the pruned-answer report read a NEIGHBOUR's relevance mask (the masks are index-aligned to the NORMALIZED instance list, the report walked the RAW one, and Stage 1 drops empty rows and re-indexes); `fixed`-prefill hidden values began reaching the payload, so a server-authored value the keyer never saw was reported as an answer that "was not saved"; and the error-summary jump links pointed at the id the GUEST runtime stamps, which this page never renders, so a jump changed step and then did nothing. **The finding to carry is methodological: the first regression test written for the mask defect PASSED against the broken code** — with one surviving and one pruned row the two implementations name different rows and render the identical string. Two pruned rows separate them by count. Every regression test was re-run against the unfixed code before being believed, and that is the only reason it was caught. Doc #27 → v1.3 (C1–C5), with reconciliations to the UX flow doc, the data dictionary, the testing strategy (including its stale "mutation testing — not currently planned" line), the R3 mirror-pair inventory and the parity matrix.

**2026-08-02 (fourth session) — H21d1 MERGED (PR #83 `774b9ae`, squash, 6/6 CI green after two red cycles, both of which were real defects rather than flakes) — the read-derived logic canvas; H-map row 254 (`ui, XL` there, split by H1f into H21d1 / H21d2, and only H21d1 ships here).** The prompt said "take H21d" and the first decision was to take HALF of it: H21d is the H-map's only XL row, H1f had already split it, and the read half is complete on its own — a canvas that writes nothing needs no serializer, no write endpoint and no 409 surface, so the split falls on a real seam rather than an arbitrary one. Three decisions were locked with the user before any code: the canvas lives as a `Structure ⇄ Logic` toggle in the builder's centre pane (one store, live, and selecting a node drives the existing config panel); predicates render as raw text AND plain English with a hard fallback; scope is H21d1 only.

**The load-bearing architectural decision is the split between what is derived where.** The rail — node order, each raw predicate, its reading, its `${key}` chips, its syntax errors — is derived CLIENT-side from the live store and re-parsed between keystrokes with the TypeScript `ExpressionParser` off the engine barrel (the second crossing from `resources/js/` into `resources/public-runtime/`, after H21c's `Encode.vue`). The three §6 graph notices stay on the SERVER behind a new read-only `GET /forms/{form}/graph` sidecar, on the ScopeNode impact/grants precedent, refetched after `store.whenIdle()` settles. **Porting `StepGraphInspector` to TypeScript was rejected outright** — it would have been a sixth R3-style mirror pair with no corpus standing over it, the hazard Doc #27 §9 and amendment A6 argue against at length. The price is a one-save lag, and it is SHOWN (a "rechecking" line; a failed check that says so) rather than hidden, because a stale clean bill of health that looks current is the failure worth avoiding. The refetch is keyed on a logic FINGERPRINT (keys, sequences, containment, expressions) rather than the whole store: a label edit cannot create or clear a cycle.

**A LIVE DEFECT WAS FOUND WHILE PLANNING AND FIXED ON THE WAY IN.** H21d1 is the first caller to run `StepGraphInspector` against an unvalidated DRAFT, and only one of its three checks was safe there. `referencesIn()` catches `Throwable` with a docblock naming H21d1 as the reason; **`emptyAtOpen()` did not**, reaching `ExpressionParser::parse()` by a longer road through `SemanticValidator` → `ExpressionEvaluator::evaluateBoolean()`. Unreachable on the publish path (`ExpressionValidationGate` refuses first, inside `publish()`), which is why it never fired in H21a — and the ORDINARY case against a draft, where a half-typed condition is what an author has most of the time. Both settle loops can raise it (sections are swept separately from fields), so the guard wraps the whole evaluation and both arms are pinned separately. **Both regression tests were run against the unfixed code first and both threw the predicted `ExpressionSyntaxException`** — H21c's standing lesson, applied to a defect found while planning rather than while reviewing.

**The notices became addressable rather than being copied.** `notices()` is now the primary method returning `GraphNotice` records naming the nodes each is about; `warnings()` is the flash-shaped grouping wrapper, reproducing H21a's three sentences byte-for-byte — now pinned in FULL rather than by needle, since the pre-H21d1 cases assert with `toContain`, which cannot see a dropped clause (and Pest's `toContain` takes varargs needles, the H17 trap). The record carries TWO strings by design: the banner wants one line per kind joining every instance, the canvas wants each instance under its node, and the cycle case proves one string cannot be both — its joining fragment is `“a” ⇄ “b”`, which reads as nothing alone. Writing the exact-array assertion caught something the old needle-based test could not see: §4.1's own empty-at-open fixture is ALSO a containment cycle, so it emits two banner lines, not one.

**The describer refuses to paraphrase, and that refusal is the deliverable.** Every node shows its expression verbatim; where the shape can be expressed in FULL it gets a sentence beside it. One operand it cannot express makes the whole reading opaque — no partial readings, no "…and 2 more". Grouping is preserved rather than flattened, because `(A or B) and C` and `A or (B and C)` are different conditions and rendering both the same way is a confident wrong answer about a form that decides who is asked what. That classifier is exactly the predicate **H21d2** needs for §8's "non-representable ⇒ read-only, never rewritten", built once instead of twice. Nothing here PRINTS an expression — prose is not syntax — so the AST→text printer H21d2 owes still does not exist.

**Three findings from the gates, each caught by a different one and each real.** (a) A `var(--mds-type-body-font-size)` that does not exist, reported by `token-references` — **which fails as a timeout in the full Vitest run**, the known flake, so it was visible only on the isolated re-run. That is the whole reason the standing practice says to re-run it there. (b) A genuine dark-mode `color-contrast` violation in new copy: `--mds-color-danger-text` is `danger-300` in dark, 4.22:1 on `bg-surface` at 13px; the `status-*-fg` family is the on-surface pair (8.43:1 / 6.39:1). (c) The mutation pass surfaced raw ASCII separator bytes (0x1D/0x1E/0x1F) sitting in the fingerprint source — functionally correct and actually the right delimiting, but invisible in an editor; now written as ``-style escapes with the reason.

**One test was CORRECTED rather than made to pass.** A comparison of two fields (`${end} > ${start}`) had been written as an opaque case on a wrong premise; it reads unambiguously, so the describer's behaviour was right and the test was wrong.

**Eleven mutations, every one reddening a disjoint set** — four PHP (drop the draft-safety catch → 3 tests; keep one fragment per kind; name only the cycle's entry node; read the published version instead of the draft) and seven TypeScript (partial chain reading; flattened grouping; the empty-section `every()` accident; section keys dropped from the label lookup; skipping `whenIdle()`; blanking notices on a failed check; widening the fingerprint to labels).

**Gates, all in-container: Pest 1846 (+13), zero failures; PHPStan L8 55 — exactly the documented local baseline, zero in any H21d1-touched file** (one `arrayValues.list` error appeared and was fixed, so the count returned to baseline rather than being waived); Pint 794; `composer audit` clean; controller-gate / migration-lint / job-payload-lint; vue-tsc both tsconfigs; **Vitest 791 (+63)** (the 2 failures are the known `token-references` full-suite timeout flake, verified passing in isolation — after it caught the real dangling token above); Vite build — **the admin bundle is unchanged at 141.66 kB and the parser lands in the lazily-resolved `Builder` page chunk (69.11 → 79.40 kB), measured against `main` rather than asserted**; `openapi.json` drift-free; **corpora byte-untouched — `grammar_version` still exactly 296 sites, the validation manifest still 114 vectors, `git diff tests/golden` empty**. **Playwright: the 12 new logic-view scans are green** (6 `builder-axe` + 6 `responsive-axe`, three viewports × light/dark); a full `builder-axe` run is 28/30, and both failures are the pre-existing dark `.mds-button--primary` contrast **verified to reproduce identically on `main`**, so neither is this branch's.

**Docs: no new number** (the H-map gives H21d no docs row) — Doc #27 → **v1.4** with amendments D1–D5, plus reconciliations to `testing-strategy.md` (H21d1's five suites, and an explicit "no sixth mirror pair" note), `architecture/technical-architecture.md` (R3: the describer and the `${key}` walk are NOT mirrors, and what WOULD have been one was deliberately not built), `feature-backlog.md` (the logic-map row shipped, with the simulator and skip-rules residues named), and `competitive-feature-parity-matrix.md` (the canvas row's read half shipped, +2 new rows).

## 2026-08-02 — H21d2 (the structured condition editor) — H-map row 254 CLOSED, the H21 arc complete

**The write half of the logic canvas**, and the increment that had to ship the AST→text printer three prior increments verified does not exist. Doc #27 → **v1.5**, amendments **E1–E5**. **MERGED PR #84 `af31026`, 6/6 CI green on the first run.**

**Three decisions locked with the user before any code.** (1) **ONE classifier, by construction** rather than two held together by a test. (2) **`ConfigPanel` replaced**, one control in two derived modes. (3) **One PR** for the whole write path, because §9's round-trip obligation is "every shape the editor can author" and a model-only PR would owe a test it could not write.

**Reusing H21d1's predicate cost more than calling it, and that is the interesting part.** `status: 'opaque'` was the right predicate but it was **fused into the prose** — `say()` returning `null` WAS "opaque", every helper below it was module-private, and the only reachable entry point took raw text, re-parsed it and computed a sentence as a side effect. There was no `isDescribable(node)`. So the classification MOVED rather than being copied: `condition-model.ts`'s `toCondition(Node) → Condition | null` is now THE classifier and `condition-describer.ts` renders its sentence FROM the returned `Condition`, which makes `say()` **total** — there is no shape it can meet and refuse, because a shape it could refuse would be a shape the editor believes it can edit.

**The price is stated rather than hidden.** The model has to be exactly as expressive as the set H21d1 shipped, quirks included: a reversed comparison (`18 < ${age}`), a field-vs-field one (`${end} > ${start}` — which H21d1 had *corrected into* the described set), a literal-vs-literal one (`1 = 1`). A tidier model — subject always a field, right side always a literal — would have been a smaller editor and would have **silently narrowed the RAIL's prose**, which is not this increment's to change. That is why both sides of a comparison are the same control: the symmetry is what the shared classifier costs. `condition-describer.test.ts` passing **byte-unchanged** across all 27 cases is the guarantee that the classification moved without its boundary moving.

**No new write endpoint, and that is how the 409 stayed inherited.** `relevant_expression` was already a key on both PATCH payloads with the row's `version` token, so the editor writes `target.relevant_expression = text; store.touch(uid, kind)` and gets the 600 ms debounce, the snapshot dirty-check, the serialized queue, the version bump, `ConflictDialog` routing and one undo entry per burst — with **zero store changes**. §9's "409 test on the new write path" is therefore **redirected, and the redirection recorded** rather than the obligation narrowed: `builderClient.test.ts` is the **first JavaScript-side coverage `builderClient` has ever had** (the typed 409 was pinned server-side only, which cannot see that the client RETURNS the conflict instead of throwing it), plus a store-level group driving a condition edit against a stubbed 409 and asserting both sides land and the stale token is NOT adopted.

**The printer checks itself, and that is load-bearing rather than defensive.** `serialize()` re-parses its own output and returns text only if it classifies back to a structurally equal `Condition`. That turns the lexer's 2000-UTF-8-byte, 500-token and 64-depth budgets into one refusal instead of three hand-maintained constants that would drift from the lexer the day either moved. It matters because **nothing validates a `relevant_expression` on write** — `UpdateFieldRequest` is shape-only and says so — so text the printer got wrong would save cleanly and meet the author at publish, in a refusal naming an expression they never typed.

**Three shapes are REFUSED rather than mangled, and each is a real limit of grammar v2.0.** A value containing BOTH `'` and `"` — `lexString` has **no escape sequences at all**, a literal runs to the first matching delimiter, so printing it with either quote would produce a *different string* silently. A number whose shortest decimal form needs exponent notation (`String(1e21)` is `"1e+21"`; `lexNumber` reads digits and at most one fractional part). A key outside `[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*`. Each surfaces inline on its row and nothing is written while it stands.

**The drift surface §8 named as option (a)'s cost is guarded by a shared fixture cross-parsed in PHP.** `tests/fixtures/condition-serializer.json` (27 `(condition, text)` cases) is driven THREE ways — TS `serialize` → text, TS `parse` → condition, an idempotence pass, and **`tests/Unit/Expressions/ConditionSerializerParityTest.php`, which parses every string with PHP's `ExpressionParser`** and checks the references land where the structured condition says. That third driver is the one that catches text only the TypeScript parser accepts. It sits outside `tests/golden/` with no `grammar_version` key and no manifest (the `step-projection.json` precedent), so **the corpora stay byte-untouched: 296 sites, 114 vectors, `git diff tests/golden` empty**. **It is NOT a sixth R3 mirror pair** — one printer, one language, PHP never prints; what is pinned is that one implementation's output is legible to both parsers. R3's inventory gains a THIRD KIND of entry: a place where syntax is *constructed*.

**Read-only constrains the rows, not the author.** §8's "renders read-only and is never rewritten" governs the structured editor; taking away the only way to edit `${age} + 1 > 18` would be a regression dressed as a safety measure, so an opaque condition keeps a fully editable textarea and the guarantee is that nothing parses, re-prints or touches a byte of it. The guarantee is **mechanical rather than careful**: emit only from a user mutation, never from a watcher, never on mount, never a value equal to the stored one — asserted over every canonical string the printer can emit, plus two describable-but-untidy shapes (`${age}   >   18` and `(${age} > 18)`) that a canonicalize-on-open implementation would quietly reformat. A half-built row is held on screen and not written (`isComplete()` gates the write), because writing the complete subtree and dropping the rest would be a silent partial save.

**A MUTATION THAT REDDENED NOTHING WAS THE MOST USEFUL RESULT, for the third increment running.** Deleting the printer's deep-equality self-check left all 106 tests green — because the length-budget case that appears to exercise it is caught one branch earlier by the re-parse *throwing*. The comparison arm had no test and is reachable: `compare eq <field> <empty text>` prints as `${a} = ''`, which the parser reads back as the **emptiness idiom**, a different condition with a different reading. Without the check, `serialize()` would hand back text meaning something other than what it was given — the silent rewrite §8 calls the disqualifier. A test now pins it, paired with the `blank` condition that legitimately prints the same string, so the refusal is a discrimination rather than a blanket rejection. Six mutations in total; the other five reddened 7, 2, 16, 6 and 8 tests respectively.

**The `token-references` flake hid a real failure again — the practice has now paid for itself twice.** The full Vitest run showed its usual two timeouts; the isolated re-run reported a dangling `--mds-color-text-primary` in the new fieldset legend. There is no such token (the family is `text-body`/`-heading`/`-secondary`/`-muted`), and an undefined custom property with no fallback invalidates the whole declaration. **Two component-test findings were real rather than test bugs**, the notable one being that the editor read its own sentence with `describe(expression)` and no `LabelLookup` — saying "age is more than 18" where the rail says "Your age is more than 18". One reading rendered two ways, which is the exact divergence the shared classifier exists to prevent; it now builds the same lookup, sections-then-fields, matching `logic-rail.ts`'s collision order.

**Gates, all in-container: Pest 1875 (+29), zero failures; PHPStan L8 55 — exactly the documented local baseline, zero in any H21d2-touched file**; Pint 795; `composer audit` clean; vue-tsc both tsconfigs; **Vitest 974 (+183)** (the 2 failures are the known `token-references` full-suite flake, verified passing in isolation); Vite build — the admin bundle is unchanged at **141.66 kB** and the editor lands in the lazily-resolved `Builder` chunk (**79.40 → 95.16 kB**); `openapi.json` verified drift-free (no migration, no `/api/v1` change). **Playwright: 12 new tests carrying 18 axe scans, all green** — 6 `builder-axe` (three viewports x light/dark, each scanning BOTH editor modes and traversing the tree by keyboard) + 6 `responsive-axe` over the seeded nested-group condition. **The full `builder-axe` matrix is 36/36** across all three viewports in both themes, which is the first time it has been clean end to end since H21d1 fixed the dark primary-fill palette. `Logic Notices Demo` gains a multi-select with real options and `(${age} > 18 or ${age} < 5) and selected(${colours}, 'red')`, so one scan sees the choice dropdown, the recursive group control and its indent rule.

**Docs: no new number** — Doc #27 → **v1.5** with amendments E1–E5, plus reconciliations to `testing-strategy.md`, `architecture/technical-architecture.md` (R3's third kind of drift surface), `feature-backlog.md` (the logic-map row's write half) and `competitive-feature-parity-matrix.md` (the workflow/branching-canvas row goes ◐ → ✓).

## 2026-08-03 — H1e (ADR-0011, the analytics substrate) — MERGED PR #85 `3d8f1f3`, 6/6 CI green first try

**Named by applying the H-map blockers, not picked.** H21d2 closed row 254 and the whole H21 arc, leaving four eligible rows once the blocked ones fell out (H22b needs H22a, H23b needs H23a): H1e, H22a, H23a, H25. H1e was the only one blocking a vertical — H24a's other three dependencies (H2, H5a, H11) were already merged — and the map's own rule front-loads docs precisely because each can reshape the increment behind it. It did.

**The reversal.** H-map row 259 specified `form_events` fed by a rate-limited unauthenticated Group-C beacon, offline-queued through Dexie, rolled up by a `MaintenanceJob`, partitioned and retention-bounded from the first migration. ADR-0011 declines all of it: `PRD.md:203-206`'s three Phase-3 acceptance criteria — cross-form aggregation, saved report views, streamed export — are satisfied by data already stored, and the alternative was a fourth unauthenticated ingress class writing the highest-write-volume table in the system onto the single self-hosted box of ADR-0005, in an area (R9) that is still open. Row 259 **and** its hazard note were rewritten in place in the H-map file so the next session cannot rebuild the beacon by accident; the two costs of the deferral (offline respondents emit no events; a relevance-filtered step graph means "step 3" is not the same node for two respondents, so branching funnel semantics is its own unsolved problem) are recorded in the ADR for Phase 4.

**Thirteen sub-decisions.** No event stream (D1); one countable-submission predicate binding on H11 and H24a alike, excluding drafts and trashed rows and forbidding a read of the typed index without joining `submissions` (D2); metadata-first aggregation with answer values opt-in and prospective-only (D3); mixed-type keys as a visible refusal naming the version rather than an `AVG` over survivors (D4); `PRD.md:198` amended to a draft-conversion rate and a median first-save→submit, both printing their denominator (D5); `forms.scope_node_id` as the grouping axis with an explicit Unassigned bucket and `nodeReach()`'s is-active semantics (D6); bounded live SQL under RLS with materialization triggered by a measured p95 breach (D7); `saved_report_views` strict-RLS with a `user_id`, never `belongs_to_user` (D8); the surface hidden not upsold, `read:analytics` new but coining no permission (D9); in-house SVG primitives, four of them, with an explicit not-built list (D10); the `--mds-chart-*` token contract, five series derived from the light+dark luminance band (D11); a data-table equivalent pinned by Vitest because axe cannot see a missing text alternative on an SVG that has an `aria-label` (D12); and the export's three load-bearing lines named so "the same pattern" is not read as "the same outer shape" (D13).

**The research earned its keep: four false statements corrected** — the fourth instance of the standing lesson. `feature-backlog.md` claimed an analytics-tab shell and chart tokens already existed (neither does, in any form). ADR-0001 and `technical-architecture.md` asserted "JSONB + GIN" as the hybrid submission model's *built* performance story; **no GIN index exists anywhere** — `submission_answers` carries one B-tree, on `(tenant_id, form_version_id)`. `data-dictionary.md` filed `scope_node_id` under §1 `tenants` while its own text said "this form"; moved to §2 `forms` along with the six schedule/save-and-resume columns that section never gained. And the gating matrix's analytics row was the one capability row that never named its code key.

**New standing gotcha — a docs-only PR can still redden CI.** `ci.yml` has no `paths:` filter, so all six jobs run in full, and two can bite on markdown: `QueuedMailContractTest` reads `docs/deployment-infrastructure.md` and asserts the `--queue=` worker order byte-exactly (the only doc-reading test in the suite, and that paragraph is exactly the queue catalog an analytics ADR wants to amend — cite `QueueName`/ADR-0007 §D6 instead), and gitleaks scans `.md` with the upstream default ruleset and no `.gitleaks.toml`.

**Docs: no new number** — ADRs share Doc #7, so `docs/PRD.md:13`'s index still tops out at #27. ADR-**0011**, not 0010: 0010 stays reserved for H1d, and the ADR-0009 reuse precedent does not transfer, because payments was cancelled (a permanent hole) while H1d is merely blocked (a temporary one). Reconciled: `PRD.md`, `feature-backlog.md`, `ux/design-system-reference.md`, `data-dictionary.md`, `form-versioning-schema-migration.md`, `non-functional-requirements.md`, `architecture/technical-architecture.md`, `adr/0001-postgresql-over-mysql.md`, `pricing-feature-gating-matrix.md`, `api-specification.md`, `multi-tenancy-rbac-design.md`, `testing-strategy.md`, `competitive-feature-parity-matrix.md`, `observability-incident-response.md`. Zero code, zero migrations, zero test files, no `openapi.json` change; golden corpora byte-untouched.

## 2026-08-03 — H24a (the analytics substrate, built) — branch `h24a-analytics-substrate`, 11 commits

**ADR-0011 implemented, not re-litigated.** No event stream, no beacon, no rollup table; aggregation over submission **metadata**, live under RLS; charting deferred to H24b. Eleven bisectable commits, the first three behaviour-neutral refactors so everything after them stands on settled ground.

**A post-approval design review found three real defects in the approved plan, all verified before folding in.** (1) `scopeCountable()` alone would NOT have satisfied §D2 — a local scope applies only at the query ROOT, so a query rooted on `submission_answer_index` inherits neither it nor the SoftDeletes global scope, which is the exact silent over-count D2 exists to prevent. (2) The `E2eSeeder` tier change would have **silently no-opped**: the block is guarded by `if (! Subscription::where('name','default')->exists())`, so CI (fresh DB) goes green while every developer's box stays on Professional and analytics 402s. It ships as an upsert, verified by re-seeding the existing dev database and reading the plan back. (3) `date_trunc('day', submitted_at)` is **not safe** — `config/database.php` sets no `timezone` key, so the session `TimeZone` GUC is whatever `postgresql.conf` says (UTC in the container, unknown on ADR-0005's box); the same row would bucket differently on different hosts and a saved view would not be reproducible. Every bucket uses the three-argument form with a validated IANA zone carried on the query object, pinned by a test asserting a 22:00 UTC row lands on the **next** day in Asia/Manila.

**TWO MUTATIONS CAME BACK GREEN, AND BOTH INDICTED THE CODE — the fourth and fifth increments running where the useful mutation result was a green one.** (a) Deleting the soft-delete half of `Submission::applyCountableJoin()` reddened nothing across 31 tests, because it had **zero callers**: the aggregator had been designed to root every query on `submissions` and join OUTWARD to the index, which is strictly better (D2 becomes structural rather than remembered) and made the helper dead. Deleted, along with a docblock that had drifted into claiming "every join goes through it". (b) Deleting `TenantContext::applyLocal()` from the export closure reddened nothing, because the rows were materialized in REQUEST scope — so all three of §D13's "load-bearing" lines were ceremony. The aggregation moved inside the closure; the mutation now reddens 2 tests. A third mutation (the permissions team-id restore) stays green and that one indicts the TEST, not the line: every analytics route's `can:` gate warms Spatie's role memo on the same User instance first, so the unwarmed case cannot be constructed today — kept, with the reasoning written into the docblock rather than deleted.

**`'can:viewAny,'.SavedReportView::class` silently authorized nothing.** `routes/api.php` has no `namespace` declaration, so with the import missing the expression compiles to the bare string `'SavedReportView'` — no exception, no warning. Laravel's `Authorize` decides class-vs-route-parameter by testing for a backslash, so it treated it as a route parameter, resolved it to `null`, and Gate denied without ever consulting a policy. Every analytics route 403'd generically, and forcing the policy to `return true` changed nothing, which is what finally pointed at the argument rather than the policy. Root cause was an import insertion that targeted a `use App\Models\Submission;` line which does not exist in that file.

**Two testing traps, now documented in the suites.** The auth guard **caches** its resolved user for the app instance, so a second `withToken()` inside one test does not re-authenticate — both tokens were valid and pointed at the right users; the second request simply kept acting as the first one's owner, turning an authorization test into one that always passes. And after a request the tenant GUC is torn down, so an in-process assertion afterwards reads "row deleted" when it means "row invisible".

**The index addendum stayed ONE, by widening rather than adding**: `submissions_analytics_series_idx` on `(tenant_id, submitted_at) INCLUDE (form_id) WHERE status <> 'draft' AND deleted_at IS NULL`, plus H12a's capacity index widened in place to `(tenant_id, form_id, submitted_at)` (a strict prefix-superset, so `FormAcceptanceGuard`'s hot-path COUNT is unaffected). `deleted_at IS NULL` is what makes an index-only scan possible, and its corollary is a standing rule: **no analytics query may use `withTrashed()` on `submissions`**. The shape is pinned **structurally, not by `EXPLAIN`** — `ScopeNodeScaleTest:120-128` already recorded that on a small table the planner tie-breaks unpredictably, so a plan-shape assertion measures the cost model rather than the property; a net-count assertion fails if a later increment quietly appends a second index.

**Other shapes worth carrying.** The subtree predicate was **extracted, not copied a third time** (`ResourceGrantResolver::subtreeFormIdsQuery()`, with `nodeReach()` as its second call site) — it was not even copyable, since `inactivePaths()` is private. §D6's scope-node axis needs **three** dispositions, not two: forms on a deactivated node fold into *Unassigned* rather than being dropped, or `sum(buckets) < total` on one screen. §D5's denominator is restricted to `source IN (guest, manual)` — a forward-dated defect fixed before it lands, since `SubmissionDraftService` is a service seam and every OCR-staged draft would otherwise turn "median first-save→submit" into encoder review latency. `AnalyticsFieldEligibility` is a `default`-less 31-case match, **verified by mutation** to produce `match.unhandled` at PHPStan L8. Both `hidden` and `calculated` are Indexable — classified by what the projector actually does, not by what feels like respondent data.

**Gates:** **Pest 1961 (+86 over H21d2's 1875), ZERO failures, 6998 assertions** (the 2 warnings are pre-existing); PHPStan L8 at **exactly 55**, the documented baseline, zero introduced (9 `User|null` errors appeared and were narrowed with the `DashboardController` device). Pint clean; `controller-gate` 59 files (parsing lives in FormRequests, which it does not scan); `migration-lint` 40 files; `openapi.json` regenerated and Redocly-clean — the diff includes the **ability enum** in the token-mint schema, not only the eight new paths. `QueuedMailContractTest` re-run green (docs touched, and `deployment-infrastructure.md` deliberately untouched).

## 2026-08-03 — H24a MERGED (PR #86 `5f9401d`, 6/6 CI green on the first run)

Closed the one gate that had never run on this branch, opened the PR, and merged. **Pest 1962 passed** (7020 assertions, +87), **PHPStan 0 on CI**, **Playwright 260 passed / 1 flaky**, contract-tests clean. One defect fixed on the way in: `DashboardController`'s comment claimed the `trends` prop was "pinned by a Pest assertion" and it was not — coverage was service-level only, so a controller-side regression would have stayed invisible to every gate until H24b. Now pinned key-by-key at the response layer, on a tenant with no subscription, which holds ADR-0011 §D5's ungated line at the same time.

**FOUR OPERATIONAL FACTS THIS SESSION COST REAL TIME TO LEARN — they are in the resume prompt, and they are the reason a "green locally" claim needs qualifying.**

**`.env` sets `CENTRAL_DOMAIN=localhost` and `phpunit.xml` does not override it.** A plain in-container `vendor/bin/pest` therefore fails **344 tests** with `NotASubdomainException: Hostname acme.meridian.test does not include a subdomain` — every test that drives a tenant URL. The full suite must run as `docker exec -e CENTRAL_DOMAIN=meridian.test …`. CI is immune because it copies `.env.example`, which sets no `CENTRAL_DOMAIN` and so defaults to `meridian.test`. The 344 failures were purely environmental and CI passed the identical suite.

**Re-seeding does NOT heal Playwright fixture drift.** `E2eSeeder`'s blocks are `doesntExist()`-guarded, so it skips any form that already exists and never resets its fields. The stateful specs — builder's keyboard grab/move/drop reorder, the library insert, the scopes re-root — had accumulated across sessions until `Community Health Survey` held **4 fields in scrambled order** (`Whole number` promoted to first, plus a `Full name` inserted by an earlier run), which made the library insert collide with `form_fields_tenant_id_form_version_id_key_unique` and select nothing. Cost: a **1.1-hour run with 9 failures**, none of them code. Only `migrate:fresh --force` + `db:seed --class=E2eSeeder` clears it — which is exactly why CI never sees this, it provisions a new database every run. After the reset the same suite ran **260/261 in 32 minutes**.

**PHPStan locally reports 55 and CI reports 0, and there is no baseline file** — so "55, zero introduced" is not a comparison against a recorded baseline, it is a local artifact, and a single genuine error would fail the job. Verified per-file instead of by count: the only two touched files carrying errors have them at lines the diff never reaches (`SubmissionInboxPresenter` L143/L330 against a hunk at L62; `E2eSeeder` L102 against hunks from L105), and every new analytics file is clean.

**The axe specs carry racy theme / live-region assertions.** This session produced exactly one flaky test in CI and exactly one locally, and **they were different tests** — CI's was `field-library-axe` "Library picker — dark", the local one `scopes-axe`'s keyboard-move live region. The local one was pinned down properly rather than waved through: the persisted state it guards was correct (`Movable District` at depth 0, root), it reproduced **0/3** on isolated re-run in the exact state that had failed, and across two full runs the failing pass moved between viewport projects from identical database state. CI's `retries: 1` absorbs these; local `retries: 0` does not.

**The tz-enum landmine did not fire, and is now a filed backlog row rather than a surprise.** `AnalyticsReportRequest:52`'s `Rule::in(DateTimeZone::listIdentifiers())` puts a **419-entry enum × 4 endpoints** (~1,676 lines, ~20% of the spec) into `openapi.json`, which `contract-tests` byte-diffs. Local PHP (`php:8.4-fpm-alpine` 8.4.23, tzdata 2026.1) and CI's **floating** `shivammathur/setup-php` `8.4` agreed today. A future PHP patch bumping timelib turns the job red on a diff nobody authored — the fix is a closure rule, ~5 lines, identical runtime behaviour.

Also worth carrying: `gh` is not pre-authed (`GH_TOKEN` from `git credential fill`), and the resulting token lacks `read:org`, so **`gh pr edit` fails** — use `gh api -X PATCH repos/OWNER/REPO/pulls/N -F body=@file`. Local Playwright needs `public/hot` removed and a build inside the node container, browsers live on the Windows host (not in any container), and `node_modules/.bin/playwright` is a broken POSIX symlink so it must be invoked as `node node_modules/@playwright/test/cli.js`. Golden corpora byte-untouched: `grammar_version` at 296 sites, `git diff tests/golden` empty. Next: **H24b**, the analytics UI — the only row H24a unblocks.

## 2026-08-03 — H24b SPLIT; H24b1 (chart primitives + chart tokens + the dashboard that reads its own data) — branch `h24b1-chart-primitives`

**H24b was split at the H21d seam before any code was written.** Its real scope — four design-system SVG primitives, a `--mds-chart-*` token family with a mandatory dark re-point, the ungated dashboard, a Business-gated `/analytics` page with filters/saved views/question explorer/export, an `E2eSeeder` submission fixture and five test surfaces — is XL, and the two halves answer two different PRD promises (`:197` Phase-1, ungated; `:203-206` Phase-3, Business). **H24b1** is the design system plus the dashboard: no route, no gate, no migration, no seeder change, no `/api/v1` touch, so `openapi.json` is byte-identical. **H24b2** is the gated page, with its scope recorded verbatim in the resume prompt.

**The five series colours are derived, and the derivation is now arithmetic in a test rather than a claim in prose.** ADR-0011 §D11 states "five is derived, not chosen" and deliberately prints no ratios, assigning the measured table to this increment. It is in DSR §4.1, both themes, computed not estimated — and the load-bearing figure is not in the table: take the light column unchanged onto the dark ground and **four of the five fail WCAG 1.4.11** (2.51 / 1.83 / **1.56** / 2.86:1, only vermilion surviving at 3.51). From the other end, the raw Okabe-Ito anchors fail white (`#E69F00` = 2.25:1). A single token set would have to satisfy both columns at once, and that band is too narrow for five distinguishable marks. `theme-overrides.test.ts` recomputes every row from `tokens/chart.json` and the CSS each run, and gained a second assertion the pre-existing accent guard would have missed: that guard tested only the five `--mds-color-*` families, so a `[data-accent]` rule recolouring a data series would have slipped straight through.

**Three shapes fell out of the arithmetic rather than taste.** `--mds-chart-other/-grid/-axis` alias **neutral primitives**, so they need no dark re-point at all — the dark block already flips the ramp beneath them, and only the five series are re-pointed, from `--mds-_chart-dark-1…5` defined once on `:root` (G11's private-custom device, same anti-drift reason). The **sequential scale is not emitted**: no consumer yet, and an unconsumed token is how the corpus grew a false "chart tokens exist" claim in the first place. And **a token name is never interpolated into `var()`** — `var(--mds-chart-series-${n})` makes `token-references.test.ts` hunt for a token literally named `--mds-chart-series-` and fail, so series colour is a static CSS class per index, which also keeps `stroke-dasharray` off presentation attributes.

**The §D12 table is the gate; axe is not.** 163 Storybook stories scanned clean — which proves nothing about the text alternative, because axe cannot detect a *missing* one behind a plausible `aria-label`. Each primitive's data table is pinned by Vitest instead. Two more structural decisions: the **value domain always includes zero** and callers cannot switch it off (a truncated axis is a claim the chart makes on its own initiative), and a **degenerate domain is the ordinary path** — a quiet 30-day window is all zeros, so `max − min` is 0 and every consumer divides by it; `niceTicks` widens rather than returning one tick, because the alternative is `d="M0 NaN …"`, which draws nothing, throws nothing and fails no gate. The time-series canvas is stretched (`preserveAspectRatio="none"` + `vector-effect="non-scaling-stroke"`) with **no text inside the SVG**, so axis labels are HTML on a CSS grid, the y-column bleeding half a line-height at each end so a tick label's *centre* lands on its gridline; the bar chart's category label is HTML beside a one-`<rect>` SVG, because SVG text neither wraps nor ellipsises and a long form title at 375px is the non-wrapping-row failure `responsive-axe` has caught three times.

**ONE DEFECT WAS FOUND BY LOOKING AT THE RENDERED PAGE WITH EVERY GATE GREEN.** With six saved drafts and none yet converted, the conversion tile read "0% — of 6 saved drafts" while the median tile, sharing one sentence, read "No drafts were explicitly saved in this period" — two tiles side by side disagreeing about whether drafts exist. `conversion_rate` is null only at a zero denominator; `median_seconds` is *additionally* null when the denominator is positive but nothing converted, because `percentile_cont` over an empty set returns NULL. That is ADR-0011 §D5's "three states, never two" landing on the UI side of the same line. **The regression test was run against the unfixed component first and genuinely reddened** (the H21c lesson) before being believed, and `resources/js/Pages/dashboard.test.ts` now pins all four prop traps.

**A recorded flake was fixed rather than tolerated.** `token-references` has timed out intermittently since H21b, and H21d1 found a REAL dangling token behind one such timeout — so "it timed out again" was never safe to wave through. It is a synchronous filesystem walk of two trees, ~2 s in isolation and 4-8 s under a loaded container, against Vitest's 5 s default. This increment adds files to the very tree it walks, so leaving the budget marginal would have pushed an already-dangerous flake toward failing on load rather than on a defect; it now carries an explicit 30 s timeout.

**The backend change is one method, and its file placement is the decision.** `top_forms.rows` carried a bare form UUID with no name. The title is resolved in `DashboardMetricsService`, never in `AnalyticsMetricsService::breakdown()` — that shape is the `/api/v1/analytics/report` response, byte-diffed against `openapi.json` and asserted key-by-key by `AnalyticsApiTest`. The lookup uses **`withTrashed()`**: `AnalyticsFormSet::visible()` roots an org-wide reader on `Form::withTrashed()`, so a soft-deleted form legitimately appears in a breakdown whose submissions are still countable, and a plain lookup would leave a bar with a number and no name.

**Gates.** Pest **1965 passed / 0 failures** (7043 assertions, +3 over H24a's 1962; the 2 warnings are pre-existing) · Vitest **1064 passed** (+66) · `vue-tsc` clean · Pint clean (826 files) · PHPStan **exactly 55, the documented local baseline, none in a file this diff touches** · `controller-gate` 59 · `migration-lint` 40 · Storybook axe **163 stories / 0 violations** · Playwright **108/108**, including the Dashboard rows now scanning real charts at 375/834/1440 in both themes · `git diff openapi.json` and `git diff tests/golden` both empty, `grammar_version` still at exactly 296.

**One local-environment fact worth recording.** The merge-blocking `design-system-a11y` job cannot be run verbatim on this machine: the node container is `node:24-alpine`, so `npx playwright install` fails with `apt-get: not found`, and the shared `node_modules` is linux-installed, so the test-runner's esbuild refuses to start on the Windows host (`@esbuild/linux-x64` present, `@esbuild/win32-x64` needed). Playwright itself *does* run from the host, so the contract was driven by hand instead — every story id from `index.json`, loaded in the same preview iframe, axe-core injected, scanned at `#storybook-root`, which is exactly what `.storybook/test-runner.ts`'s `preVisit`/`postVisit` pair does. CI runs the genuine job. Note also that `npm --prefix packages/design-system install` rewrites `package-lock.json` with `"peer": true` metadata churn on this npm version — revert it, it is not part of any increment.

## 2026-08-03 — H24b1 MERGED (PR #87 `69a54bd`, 6/6 CI green on the first run)

Opened and merged with no red cycle. **Pest 1965 passed / 7043 assertions** — byte-identical to the local run, so `docker exec -e CENTRAL_DOMAIN=meridian.test …` reproduced CI exactly this time rather than approximately. **Playwright 261 passed in 9.5 min with no flaky**: the axe races that produced one flaky test in H24a's merge run did not recur, and the Dashboard rows now scan real charts. **Contract-tests clean**, confirming `openapi.json` is untouched by a UI-only increment.

**The `design-system-a11y` job reported 163 tests passed — the same 163 the host-driven stand-in reported, story for story.** That is worth recording as evidence rather than coincidence: the local substitution (drive every story id from `index.json` through the same preview iframe, inject axe-core, scan `#storybook-root`) reproduces the real runner's coverage exactly, so it is a usable local gate on this machine and not merely a gesture at one. It remains a stand-in — the alpine container cannot install Playwright browsers and the linux-built `node_modules` blocks the runner's esbuild on Windows — but a stand-in whose count matches the genuine job is one a future session can trust the same way.

`main` is clean at `69a54bd`. The eligible set is now **H24b2** (the Business-gated `/analytics` page — the second half of the split, with its full scope in the resume prompt), **H22a** (custom-domains backend), **H23a** (tenant branding), **H25** (the R5 immutability trigger).
